{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_015cJoGF2QWjesjJ6FSRcGuZ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01TkFFqqzjjWsd8DasxCYoNm","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Envoy ext_proc HTTP Filter: Migration Guide v1.30 → v1.31\n\n## Overview\n\nEnvoy v1.31.0 (released July 19, 2024) introduces several meaningful changes to the External Processing (ext_proc) HTTP filter. The changes fall into three categories:\n\n1. **A breaking API type change** in the `ImmediateResponse` message (`body` field: `string` → `bytes`)\n2. **New configuration fields** on `ExternalProcessor` for route cache control and observability stream lifecycle management\n3. **Behavioral changes** affecting timeout error codes and gRPC tracing propagation\n\nThe `processing_mode.proto` file is unchanged between the two versions.\n\n**Source files examined:**\n- `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n- `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`\n- `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.h`\n- `changelogs/current.yaml` (v1.31.0)\n\n---\n\n## Breaking Changes\n\n### 1. `ImmediateResponse.body` type changed from `string` to `bytes`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`, field `ImmediateResponse.body` (field number 3)\n\n**v1.30.0:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1 [(validate.rules).message = {required: true}];\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  string body = 3;   // <-- was string\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**v1.31.0:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1 [(validate.rules).message = {required: true}];\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  bytes body = 3;    // <-- now bytes\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**Impact:** Any ext_proc server implementation that populates the `body` field of `ImmediateResponse` must be updated. In most gRPC code-generation ecosystems the wire format of `string` and `bytes` is identical (both are length-delimited), so existing Protobuf-serialized messages will still decode without error. However:\n\n- **Generated code in typed languages** (Go, Java, C++, Python with strict typing) will produce a compile or runtime type error if the field is accessed as `string` after regenerating the stubs from the new proto.\n- **Dynamic languages or JSON serialization paths** may experience encoding differences (JSON represents `bytes` as base64, whereas `string` is a plain JSON string).\n\n**Why the change?** The `body` field carries raw HTTP response body bytes, which may contain arbitrary binary data (e.g., compressed or binary content). Using `string` was incorrect because protobuf `string` requires valid UTF-8, whereas `bytes` accepts arbitrary byte sequences.\n\n**Migration action:**\n\n1. Regenerate your gRPC stubs from the v1.31 proto definitions.\n2. Update your ext_proc server code to construct the `ImmediateResponse.body` as a byte array/slice rather than a string.\n\n**Before (Go example):**\n```go\nresp := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &v3.HttpStatus{Code: v3.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   \"Access denied\",  // string\n}\n```\n\n**After (Go example):**\n```go\nresp := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &v3.HttpStatus{Code: v3.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   []byte(\"Access denied\"),  // bytes\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Deprecated Features\n\n### 1. `disable_clear_route_cache` superseded by `route_cache_action`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n\nThe existing boolean field `disable_clear_route_cache` (field 11) is now annotated with a migration hint indicating it will be promoted into a `oneof` group (`clear_route_cache_type`) alongside the new `route_cache_action` field. The documentation comment in v1.31 explicitly states:\n\n> \"It is recommended to set `route_cache_action` which supersedes `disable_clear_route_cache`. Only one of `disable_clear_route_cache` or `route_cache_action` can be set.\"\n\n**v1.30.0:**\n```protobuf\nbool disable_clear_route_cache = 11;\n```\n\n**v1.31.0:**\n```protobuf\nbool disable_clear_route_cache = 11\n    [(udpa.annotations.field_migrate).oneof_promotion = \"clear_route_cache_type\"];\n\nRouteCacheAction route_cache_action = 18\n    [(udpa.annotations.field_migrate).oneof_promotion = \"clear_route_cache_type\"];\n```\n\nThe `disable_clear_route_cache = true` configuration is equivalent to setting `route_cache_action = RETAIN`. Setting both fields simultaneously is not permitted.\n\n**Migration advice:** Replace `disable_clear_route_cache: true` with `route_cache_action: RETAIN` in your filter configuration. Remove `disable_clear_route_cache` from any configuration that also sets `route_cache_action`.\n\n---\n\n## New Features\n\n### 1. New `RouteCacheAction` enum on `ExternalProcessor`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n\nA new enum `RouteCacheAction` and corresponding field `route_cache_action` (field 18) provide finer-grained control over route cache behavior when request headers are processed by the external processor.\n\n```protobuf\nenum RouteCacheAction {\n  // Clear the route cache only when clear_route_cache is set in the ext_proc response (default).\n  DEFAULT = 0;\n\n  // Always clear the route cache, regardless of the clear_route_cache bit.\n  CLEAR = 1;\n\n  // Never clear the route cache, regardless of the clear_route_cache bit.\n  // Equivalent to disable_clear_route_cache = true.\n  RETAIN = 2;\n}\n\nRouteCacheAction route_cache_action = 18;\n```\n\n**Example configuration (YAML):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n  - name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n    typed_config:\n      \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n      grpc_service:\n        envoy_grpc:\n          cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n      # New: always clear the route cache after processing request headers\n      route_cache_action: CLEAR\n```\n\n**When to use each value:**\n- `DEFAULT` — Keep existing behaviour; the external processor signals when to clear the cache via the `clear_route_cache` field in its response.\n- `CLEAR` — Use when your ext_proc server always modifies routing headers (e.g., `:authority`, `x-forwarded-host`) and you want to guarantee the route is re-evaluated.\n- `RETAIN` — Use when you want to prevent route re-evaluation even if the ext_proc server requests it (e.g., for security or performance reasons). Replaces `disable_clear_route_cache: true`.\n\n**Behavioral note (changelog):** Adding `route_cache_action` is recorded as a *behavior change* in the v1.31 changelog, meaning its introduction actively changes how configurations that had `disable_clear_route_cache` are interpreted and encouraged to migrate.\n\n---\n\n### 2. New `deferred_close_timeout` field on `ExternalProcessor`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, field 19\n\n```protobuf\n// Specifies the deferred closure timeout for the gRPC stream that connects to the external\n// processor. Currently used only in observability_mode.\n// Default: 5000 milliseconds (5 seconds).\ngoogle.protobuf.Duration deferred_close_timeout = 19;\n```\n\nThis field controls how long Envoy holds open an observability-mode gRPC stream after the client-to-backend stream has completed. In observability mode, the ext_proc gRPC stream may outlive the regular HTTP transaction (because the ext_proc server does not respond to each message). This timeout prevents streams from being held open indefinitely.\n\n**Default:** 5000 ms (5 seconds). Introduced alongside the `observability_mode` feature; if you use `observability_mode: true`, consider tuning this value based on your ext_proc server's processing latency.\n\n**Example configuration (YAML):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n  - name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n    typed_config:\n      \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n      grpc_service:\n        envoy_grpc:\n          cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n      observability_mode: true\n      deferred_close_timeout: \"10s\"  # Hold streams open up to 10 seconds\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. `observability_mode` formally supported with deferred stream lifecycle management\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, field 17 (present in v1.30); implementation infrastructure added in v1.31\n\nThe `observability_mode` field existed in v1.30 but in v1.31 received full implementation support including:\n\n- A new `ThreadLocalStreamManager` class (`source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.h`) that tracks active observability-mode streams across threads.\n- A new `DeferredDeletableStream` struct that wraps the gRPC stream and closes it after `deferred_close_timeout` elapses.\n- The `encodeComplete()` callback now calls `logGrpcStreamInfo()` in observability mode.\n- Helper methods `sendHeadersInObservabilityMode()` and `sendDataInObservabilityMode()` were added to the `Filter` class.\n\nThe changelog entry for this feature states:\n\n> \"Added support for observability mode which deprecates `async_mode`. If enabled, each part of the HTTP request or response specified by `ProcessingMode` is sent without waiting for the response from the ext_proc service. It is 'Send and Go' mode that can be used by external processor to observe Envoy data and status.\"\n\n**In observability mode (v1.31 behavior):**\n1. Only `STREAMED` body processing mode is supported; other body modes are ignored.\n2. The ext_proc server must not send back processing responses; responses are silently ignored.\n3. The `message_timeout` restriction does not apply.\n4. The ext_proc server may close the stream to indicate no more messages are needed.\n5. After the HTTP transaction completes, Envoy holds the gRPC stream open for up to `deferred_close_timeout`.\n\n---\n\n## Behavioral Changes\n\n### 1. Timeout errors now return `504 Gateway Timeout` instead of `500 Internal Server Error`\n\n**Area:** `ext_proc` (changelog `minor_behavior_changes`)\n\nWhen the ext_proc message timeout fires (controlled by `message_timeout`), Envoy previously returned `500 Internal Server Error` to the downstream client. As of v1.31.0, it returns `504 Gateway Timeout`.\n\n**Impact:** Downstream clients or monitoring systems that inspect HTTP status codes to differentiate between internal errors and gateway timeouts will observe this change automatically. No configuration change is required, but you should update:\n- Alert rules that trigger on `500` errors specifically for ext_proc timeouts.\n- Integration tests that assert a specific response code when ext_proc times out.\n\n---\n\n### 2. gRPC tracing context headers are now propagated in ext_proc streams\n\n**Area:** `grpc` / `ext_proc` (changelog `minor_behavior_changes`)\n\nPreviously, tracing context headers (e.g., `traceparent`, `x-b3-traceid`) were not propagated when calling the ext_proc external service via either the Envoy gRPC client or the Google gRPC client. As of v1.31.0, these headers are correctly propagated.\n\n**Impact:** If you use distributed tracing (Zipkin, Jaeger, OpenTelemetry, Datadog), ext_proc calls will now appear as child spans in traces. This is generally desirable. However, be aware that:\n- Your ext_proc server will now receive tracing headers it previously did not see.\n- If your ext_proc server forwards requests upstream, it should propagate these headers to maintain trace continuity.\n\n---\n\n## Migration Steps\n\nFollow these steps in order when upgrading from Envoy v1.30.0 to v1.31.0:\n\n### Step 1: Update `ImmediateResponse.body` in your ext_proc server\n\n1. Regenerate gRPC stubs from the updated proto files (specifically `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`).\n2. Update all code that sets `ImmediateResponse.body` to use `bytes` instead of `string`.\n3. If you serialize `ImmediateResponse` to JSON (e.g., for logging or testing), update your deserialization logic to handle the field as base64-encoded bytes rather than a plain UTF-8 string.\n\n### Step 2: Migrate `disable_clear_route_cache` to `route_cache_action`\n\nReplace:\n```yaml\n# v1.30 configuration\ntyped_config:\n  \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n  disable_clear_route_cache: true\n```\n\nWith:\n```yaml\n# v1.31 recommended configuration\ntyped_config:\n  \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n  route_cache_action: RETAIN\n```\n\nIf you were using `disable_clear_route_cache: false` (or omitting it, since `false` is the default), no change is needed — that maps to `route_cache_action: DEFAULT`, which is also the default.\n\n**Important:** Do not set both `disable_clear_route_cache` and `route_cache_action` simultaneously. The proto annotations (`oneof_promotion`) indicate these fields are mutually exclusive.\n\n### Step 3: Configure `deferred_close_timeout` if using observability mode\n\nIf you use `observability_mode: true`, add a `deferred_close_timeout` appropriate for your workload:\n\n```yaml\ntyped_config:\n  \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n  observability_mode: true\n  deferred_close_timeout:\n    seconds: 10   # Adjust based on ext_proc processing latency\n```\n\nIf you do not set this field, the default of 5 seconds applies.\n\n### Step 4: Update monitoring for timeout error codes\n\n- Update any dashboards, alerts, or SLO definitions that distinguish `500` errors caused by ext_proc timeouts. These will now appear as `504` errors.\n- Update integration tests that check for specific HTTP status codes when simulating ext_proc timeout scenarios.\n\n### Step 5: Verify tracing integration\n\n- If you use distributed tracing, deploy the upgraded Envoy and verify in your tracing backend that ext_proc gRPC calls now appear correctly as child spans.\n- Confirm that your ext_proc server correctly handles and forwards the incoming tracing headers (e.g., `traceparent`, `x-b3-traceid`, `x-b3-spanid`).\n\n---\n\n## Testing Recommendations\n\n### Unit and Integration Tests\n\n1. **`ImmediateResponse.body` type check:** Write a test that sends an `ImmediateResponse` with binary content (non-UTF-8 bytes) in the `body` field. Verify Envoy correctly returns that binary content in the HTTP response body.\n\n2. **Route cache action:** Test all three `RouteCacheAction` values:\n   - `DEFAULT`: Verify the route is re-evaluated only when `clear_route_cache: true` is set in the response.\n   - `CLEAR`: Verify the route is always re-evaluated after request header processing, even if `clear_route_cache` is not set.\n   - `RETAIN`: Verify the route is never re-evaluated even if the ext_proc server sets `clear_route_cache: true`.\n\n3. **Timeout response code:** Write a test that configures a short `message_timeout` and a deliberately slow ext_proc server. Verify the downstream response is `504 Gateway Timeout` (not `500`).\n\n4. **Observability mode deferred close:** Test that after an HTTP transaction completes, the gRPC stream to the ext_proc server remains open for the configured `deferred_close_timeout` duration and then closes cleanly.\n\n### Canary Deployment\n\nBefore rolling out v1.31 to production:\n\n1. Deploy Envoy v1.31 in a canary environment with a shadow copy of production traffic.\n2. Monitor ext_proc-related stats: `ext_proc.message_timeouts`, `ext_proc.streams_failed`, `ext_proc.clear_route_cache_ignored`, `ext_proc.clear_route_cache_disabled`.\n3. Check your tracing backend for newly appearing ext_proc spans and verify they are correctly parented.\n4. Verify that any `504` responses attributed to ext_proc timeouts are correctly reflected in your monitoring.\n\n### Load Testing\n\nIf you use `observability_mode`, perform load tests that create high request throughput, then verify that deferred streams do not accumulate to the point of exhausting gRPC connections or file descriptors. Tune `deferred_close_timeout` accordingly.\n\n---\n\n## Rollback Guidance\n\nIf issues occur after upgrading to v1.31.0, follow these steps to revert:\n\n### Immediate Rollback (Configuration Only)\n\nThe v1.31 ext_proc filter configuration is largely backward-compatible with v1.30 behavior:\n- `route_cache_action: DEFAULT` behaves identically to `disable_clear_route_cache: false` from v1.30 — this is the default and requires no change.\n- The `deferred_close_timeout` and `route_cache_action` fields are unknown to v1.30 and will cause a validation error if you downgrade Envoy binary without removing them from the config.\n\n**Before downgrading the Envoy binary:**\n1. Remove `route_cache_action` from your configuration and restore `disable_clear_route_cache` if needed.\n2. Remove `deferred_close_timeout` from your configuration.\n3. Regenerate gRPC stubs from the v1.30 proto files for your ext_proc server.\n4. Revert `ImmediateResponse.body` back to `string` in your ext_proc server code.\n\n### Binary Rollback\n\n1. Stop the Envoy v1.31 process.\n2. Restore the v1.30 Envoy binary.\n3. Restore the previous configuration (without v1.31-specific fields).\n4. Restart.\n\n### Timeout Error Code Rollback\n\nThe `504` vs `500` behavior change for ext_proc timeouts cannot be reverted via configuration in v1.31. If downstream clients require `500` for compatibility reasons, you will need to stay on v1.30 or handle the translation in a downstream filter or proxy layer.\n\n### Tracing Header Propagation Rollback\n\nThe gRPC tracing header propagation change also cannot be disabled via configuration in v1.31. If this causes issues (e.g., unexpected tracing headers breaking your ext_proc server), you will need to update your ext_proc server to tolerate unknown headers, or revert to v1.30.\n\n---\n\n## Summary of Changes\n\n| Category | Change | Impact |\n|----------|--------|--------|\n| **Breaking** | `ImmediateResponse.body`: `string` → `bytes` | Requires ext_proc server code update and stub regeneration |\n| **Deprecated** | `disable_clear_route_cache` superseded by `route_cache_action` | Migrate to `route_cache_action: RETAIN`; both still work but setting both is not allowed |\n| **New Field** | `route_cache_action` (enum: DEFAULT/CLEAR/RETAIN) on `ExternalProcessor` | Optional; replaces `disable_clear_route_cache` with richer semantics |\n| **New Field** | `deferred_close_timeout` on `ExternalProcessor` | Required tuning when using `observability_mode: true` |\n| **Behavior** | Observability mode fully implemented with deferred stream management | Stable for production use; configure `deferred_close_timeout` |\n| **Behavior** | Ext_proc timeout errors return `504` instead of `500` | Update alerting, SLOs, and integration tests |\n| **Behavior** | gRPC tracing context headers now propagated to ext_proc service | Verify tracing integration; update ext_proc server if needed |\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3336,"cache_read_input_tokens":108558,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3336},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a416e006-2006-4aae-938e-2e1c74008162","uuid":"658012f8-73de-42d1-85e5-95ab2eadf4bd"}

The changes fall into three categories:\n\n1. **A breaking API type change** in the `ImmediateResponse` message (`body` field: `string` → `bytes`)\n2. **New configuration fields** on `ExternalProcessor` for route cache control and observability stream lifecycle management\n3. **Behavioral changes** affecting timeout error codes and gRPC tracing propagation\n\nThe `processing_mode.proto` file is unchanged between the two versions.\n\n**Source files examined:**\n- `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n- `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`\n- `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.h`\n- `changelogs/current.yaml` (v1.31.0)\n\n---\n\n## Breaking Changes\n\n### 1. `ImmediateResponse.body` type changed from `string` to `bytes`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`, field `ImmediateResponse.body` (field number 3)\n\n**v1.30.0:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1 [(validate.rules).message = {required: true}];\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  string body = 3;   // <-- was string\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**v1.31.0:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1 [(validate.rules).message = {required: true}];\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  bytes body = 3;    // <-- now bytes\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**Impact:** Any ext_proc server implementation that populates the `body` field of `ImmediateResponse` must be updated. In most gRPC code-generation ecosystems the wire format of `string` and `bytes` is identical (both are length-delimited), so existing Protobuf-serialized messages will still decode without error. However:\n\n- **Generated code in typed languages** (Go, Java, C++, Python with strict typing) will produce a compile or runtime type error if the field is accessed as `string` after regenerating the stubs from the new proto.\n- **Dynamic languages or JSON serialization paths** may experience encoding differences (JSON represents `bytes` as base64, whereas `string` is a plain JSON string).\n\n**Why the change?** The `body` field carries raw HTTP response body bytes, which may contain arbitrary binary data (e.g., compressed or binary content). Using `string` was incorrect because protobuf `string` requires valid UTF-8, whereas `bytes` accepts arbitrary byte sequences.\n\n**Migration action:**\n\n1. Regenerate your gRPC stubs from the v1.31 proto definitions.\n2. Update your ext_proc server code to construct the `ImmediateResponse.body` as a byte array/slice rather than a string.\n\n**Before (Go example):**\n```go\nresp := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &v3.HttpStatus{Code: v3.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   \"Access denied\",  // string\n}\n```\n\n**After (Go example):**\n```go\nresp := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &v3.HttpStatus{Code: v3.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   []byte(\"Access denied\"),  // bytes\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Deprecated Features\n\n### 1. `disable_clear_route_cache` superseded by `route_cache_action`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n\nThe existing boolean field `disable_clear_route_cache` (field 11) is now annotated with a migration hint indicating it will be promoted into a `oneof` group (`clear_route_cache_type`) alongside the new `route_cache_action` field. The documentation comment in v1.31 explicitly states:\n\n> \"It is recommended to set `route_cache_action` which supersedes `disable_clear_route_cache`. Only one of `disable_clear_route_cache` or `route_cache_action` can be set.\"\n\n**v1.30.0:**\n```protobuf\nbool disable_clear_route_cache = 11;\n```\n\n**v1.31.0:**\n```protobuf\nbool disable_clear_route_cache = 11\n    [(udpa.annotations.field_migrate).oneof_promotion = \"clear_route_cache_type\"];\n\nRouteCacheAction route_cache_action = 18\n    [(udpa.annotations.field_migrate).oneof_promotion = \"clear_route_cache_type\"];\n```\n\nThe `disable_clear_route_cache = true` configuration is equivalent to setting `route_cache_action = RETAIN`. Setting both fields simultaneously is not permitted.\n\n**Migration advice:** Replace `disable_clear_route_cache: true` with `route_cache_action: RETAIN` in your filter configuration. Remove `disable_clear_route_cache` from any configuration that also sets `route_cache_action`.\n\n---\n\n## New Features\n\n### 1. New `RouteCacheAction` enum on `ExternalProcessor`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n\nA new enum `RouteCacheAction` and corresponding field `route_cache_action` (field 18) provide finer-grained control over route cache behavior when request headers are processed by the external processor.\n\n```protobuf\nenum RouteCacheAction {\n  // Clear the route cache only when clear_route_cache is set in the ext_proc response (default).\n  DEFAULT = 0;\n\n  // Always clear the route cache, regardless of the clear_route_cache bit.\n  CLEAR = 1;\n\n  // Never clear the route cache, regardless of the clear_route_cache bit.\n  // Equivalent to disable_clear_route_cache = true.\n  RETAIN = 2;\n}\n\nRouteCacheAction route_cache_action = 18;\n```\n\n**Example configuration (YAML):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n  - name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n    typed_config:\n      \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n      grpc_service:\n        envoy_grpc:\n          cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n      # New: always clear the route cache after processing request headers\n      route_cache_action: CLEAR\n```\n\n**When to use each value:**\n- `DEFAULT` — Keep existing behaviour; the external processor signals when to clear the cache via the `clear_route_cache` field in its response.\n- `CLEAR` — Use when your ext_proc server always modifies routing headers (e.g., `:authority`, `x-forwarded-host`) and you want to guarantee the route is re-evaluated.\n- `RETAIN` — Use when you want to prevent route re-evaluation even if the ext_proc server requests it (e.g., for security or performance reasons). Replaces `disable_clear_route_cache: true`.\n\n**Behavioral note (changelog):** Adding `route_cache_action` is recorded as a *behavior change* in the v1.31 changelog, meaning its introduction actively changes how configurations that had `disable_clear_route_cache` are interpreted and encouraged to migrate.\n\n---\n\n### 2. New `deferred_close_timeout` field on `ExternalProcessor`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, field 19\n\n```protobuf\n// Specifies the deferred closure timeout for the gRPC stream that connects to the external\n// processor. Currently used only in observability_mode.\n// Default: 5000 milliseconds (5 seconds).\ngoogle.protobuf.Duration deferred_close_timeout = 19;\n```\n\nThis field controls how long Envoy holds open an observability-mode gRPC stream after the client-to-backend stream has completed. In observability mode, the ext_proc gRPC stream may outlive the regular HTTP transaction (because the ext_proc server does not respond to each message). This timeout prevents streams from being held open indefinitely.\n\n**Default:** 5000 ms (5 seconds). Introduced alongside the `observability_mode` feature; if you use `observability_mode: true`, consider tuning this value based on your ext_proc server's processing latency.\n\n**Example configuration (YAML):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n  - name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n    typed_config:\n      \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n      grpc_service:\n        envoy_grpc:\n          cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n      observability_mode: true\n      deferred_close_timeout: \"10s\"  # Hold streams open up to 10 seconds\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. `observability_mode` formally supported with deferred stream lifecycle management\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, field 17 (present in v1.30); implementation infrastructure added in v1.31\n\nThe `observability_mode` field existed in v1.30 but in v1.31 received full implementation support including:\n\n- A new `ThreadLocalStreamManager` class (`source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.h`) that tracks active observability-mode streams across threads.\n- A new `DeferredDeletableStream` struct that wraps the gRPC stream and closes it after `deferred_close_timeout` elapses.\n- The `encodeComplete()` callback now calls `logGrpcStreamInfo()` in observability mode.\n- Helper methods `sendHeadersInObservabilityMode()` and `sendDataInObservabilityMode()` were added to the `Filter` class.\n\nThe changelog entry for this feature states:\n\n> \"Added support for observability mode which deprecates `async_mode`. If enabled, each part of the HTTP request or response specified by `ProcessingMode` is sent without waiting for the response from the ext_proc service. It is 'Send and Go' mode that can be used by external processor to observe Envoy data and status.\"\n\n**In observability mode (v1.31 behavior):**\n1. Only `STREAMED` body processing mode is supported; other body modes are ignored.\n2. The ext_proc server must not send back processing responses; responses are silently ignored.\n3. The `message_timeout` restriction does not apply.\n4. The ext_proc server may close the stream to indicate no more messages are needed.\n5. After the HTTP transaction completes, Envoy holds the gRPC stream open for up to `deferred_close_timeout`.\n\n---\n\n## Behavioral Changes\n\n### 1. Timeout errors now return `504 Gateway Timeout` instead of `500 Internal Server Error`\n\n**Area:** `ext_proc` (changelog `minor_behavior_changes`)\n\nWhen the ext_proc message timeout fires (controlled by `message_timeout`), Envoy previously returned `500 Internal Server Error` to the downstream client. As of v1.31.0, it returns `504 Gateway Timeout`.\n\n**Impact:** Downstream clients or monitoring systems that inspect HTTP status codes to differentiate between internal errors and gateway timeouts will observe this change automatically. No configuration change is required, but you should update:\n- Alert rules that trigger on `500` errors specifically for ext_proc timeouts.\n- Integration tests that assert a specific response code when ext_proc times out.\n\n---\n\n### 2. gRPC tracing context headers are now propagated in ext_proc streams\n\n**Area:** `grpc` / `ext_proc` (changelog `minor_behavior_changes`)\n\nPreviously, tracing context headers (e.g., `traceparent`, `x-b3-traceid`) were not propagated when calling the ext_proc external service via either the Envoy gRPC client or the Google gRPC client. As of v1.31.0, these headers are correctly propagated.\n\n**Impact:** If you use distributed tracing (Zipkin, Jaeger, OpenTelemetry, Datadog), ext_proc calls will now appear as child spans in traces. This is generally desirable. However, be aware that:\n- Your ext_proc server will now receive tracing headers it previously did not see.\n- If your ext_proc server forwards requests upstream, it should propagate these headers to maintain trace continuity.\n\n---\n\n## Migration Steps\n\nFollow these steps in order when upgrading from Envoy v1.30.0 to v1.31.0:\n\n### Step 1: Update `ImmediateResponse.body` in your ext_proc server\n\n1. Regenerate gRPC stubs from the updated proto files (specifically `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`).\n2. Update all code that sets `ImmediateResponse.body` to use `bytes` instead of `string`.\n3. If you serialize `ImmediateResponse` to JSON (e.g., for logging or testing), update your deserialization logic to handle the field as base64-encoded bytes rather than a plain UTF-8 string.\n\n### Step 2: Migrate `disable_clear_route_cache` to `route_cache_action`\n\nReplace:\n```yaml\n# v1.30 configuration\ntyped_config:\n  \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n  disable_clear_route_cache: true\n```\n\nWith:\n```yaml\n# v1.31 recommended configuration\ntyped_config:\n  \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n  route_cache_action: RETAIN\n```\n\nIf you were using `disable_clear_route_cache: false` (or omitting it, since `false` is the default), no change is needed — that maps to `route_cache_action: DEFAULT`, which is also the default.\n\n**Important:** Do not set both `disable_clear_route_cache` and `route_cache_action` simultaneously. The proto annotations (`oneof_promotion`) indicate these fields are mutually exclusive.\n\n### Step 3: Configure `deferred_close_timeout` if using observability mode\n\nIf you use `observability_mode: true`, add a `deferred_close_timeout` appropriate for your workload:\n\n```yaml\ntyped_config:\n  \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n  observability_mode: true\n  deferred_close_timeout:\n    seconds: 10   # Adjust based on ext_proc processing latency\n```\n\nIf you do not set this field, the default of 5 seconds applies.\n\n### Step 4: Update monitoring for timeout error codes\n\n- Update any dashboards, alerts, or SLO definitions that distinguish `500` errors caused by ext_proc timeouts. These will now appear as `504` errors.\n- Update integration tests that check for specific HTTP status codes when simulating ext_proc timeout scenarios.\n\n### Step 5: Verify tracing integration\n\n- If you use distributed tracing, deploy the upgraded Envoy and verify in your tracing backend that ext_proc gRPC calls now appear correctly as child spans.\n- Confirm that your ext_proc server correctly handles and forwards the incoming tracing headers (e.g., `traceparent`, `x-b3-traceid`, `x-b3-spanid`).\n\n---\n\n## Testing Recommendations\n\n### Unit and Integration Tests\n\n1. **`ImmediateResponse.body` type check:** Write a test that sends an `ImmediateResponse` with binary content (non-UTF-8 bytes) in the `body` field. Verify Envoy correctly returns that binary content in the HTTP response body.\n\n2. **Route cache action:** Test all three `RouteCacheAction` values:\n   - `DEFAULT`: Verify the route is re-evaluated only when `clear_route_cache: true` is set in the response.\n   - `CLEAR`: Verify the route is always re-evaluated after request header processing, even if `clear_route_cache` is not set.\n   - `RETAIN`: Verify the route is never re-evaluated even if the ext_proc server sets `clear_route_cache: true`.\n\n3. **Timeout response code:** Write a test that configures a short `message_timeout` and a deliberately slow ext_proc server. Verify the downstream response is `504 Gateway Timeout` (not `500`).\n\n4. **Observability mode deferred close:** Test that after an HTTP transaction completes, the gRPC stream to the ext_proc server remains open for the configured `deferred_close_timeout` duration and then closes cleanly.\n\n### Canary Deployment\n\nBefore rolling out v1.31 to production:\n\n1. Deploy Envoy v1.31 in a canary environment with a shadow copy of production traffic.\n2. Monitor ext_proc-related stats: `ext_proc.message_timeouts`, `ext_proc.streams_failed`, `ext_proc.clear_route_cache_ignored`, `ext_proc.clear_route_cache_disabled`.\n3. Check your tracing backend for newly appearing ext_proc spans and verify they are correctly parented.\n4. Verify that any `504` responses attributed to ext_proc timeouts are correctly reflected in your monitoring.\n\n### Load Testing\n\nIf you use `observability_mode`, perform load tests that create high request throughput, then verify that deferred streams do not accumulate to the point of exhausting gRPC connections or file descriptors. Tune `deferred_close_timeout` accordingly.\n\n---\n\n## Rollback Guidance\n\nIf issues occur after upgrading to v1.31.0, follow these steps to revert:\n\n### Immediate Rollback (Configuration Only)\n\nThe v1.31 ext_proc filter configuration is largely backward-compatible with v1.30 behavior:\n- `route_cache_action: DEFAULT` behaves identically to `disable_clear_route_cache: false` from v1.30 — this is the default and requires no change.\n- The `deferred_close_timeout` and `route_cache_action` fields are unknown to v1.30 and will cause a validation error if you downgrade Envoy binary without removing them from the config.\n\n**Before downgrading the Envoy binary:**\n1. Remove `route_cache_action` from your configuration and restore `disable_clear_route_cache` if needed.\n2. Remove `deferred_close_timeout` from your configuration.\n3. Regenerate gRPC stubs from the v1.30 proto files for your ext_proc server.\n4. Revert `ImmediateResponse.body` back to `string` in your ext_proc server code.\n\n### Binary Rollback\n\n1. Stop the Envoy v1.31 process.\n2. Restore the v1.30 Envoy binary.\n3. Restore the previous configuration (without v1.31-specific fields).\n4. Restart.\n\n### Timeout Error Code Rollback\n\nThe `504` vs `500` behavior change for ext_proc timeouts cannot be reverted via configuration in v1.31. If downstream clients require `500` for compatibility reasons, you will need to stay on v1.30 or handle the translation in a downstream filter or proxy layer.\n\n### Tracing Header Propagation Rollback\n\nThe gRPC tracing header propagation change also cannot be disabled via configuration in v1.31. If this causes issues (e.g., unexpected tracing headers breaking your ext_proc server), you will need to update your ext_proc server to tolerate unknown headers, or revert to v1.30.\n\n---\n\n## Summary of Changes\n\n| Category | Change | Impact |\n|----------|--------|--------|\n| **Breaking** | `ImmediateResponse.body`: `string` → `bytes` | Requires ext_proc server code update and stub regeneration |\n| **Deprecated** | `disable_clear_route_cache` superseded by `route_cache_action` | Migrate to `route_cache_action: RETAIN`; both still work but setting both is not allowed |\n| **New Field** | `route_cache_action` (enum: DEFAULT/CLEAR/RETAIN) on `ExternalProcessor` | Optional; replaces `disable_clear_route_cache` with richer semantics |\n| **New Field** | `deferred_close_timeout` on `ExternalProcessor` | Required tuning when using `observability_mode: true` |\n| **Behavior** | Observability mode fully implemented with deferred stream management | Stable for production use; configure `deferred_close_timeout` |\n| **Behavior** | Ext_proc timeout errors return `504` instead of `500` | Update alerting, SLOs, and integration tests |\n| **Behavior** | gRPC tracing context headers now propagated to ext_proc service | Verify tracing integration; update ext_proc server if needed |\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
